It reads one CSV file, infers a type for each column, and writes either a profile table or a frequency list to standard output as CSV. Diagnostics go to standard error. Exit codes are 0 (success), 1 (operational failure), 2 (usage error), and 3 (rejected input).\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`, which remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | Every package; no third-party modules and no `require` line in `go.mod` |\n| go test and go vet | The project suite behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| CSV (RFC 4180, `encoding/csv`) | Reading input and writing output |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` |\n\nThere is no database and no persistent state, so there is no `DATABASE.md`.\n\n## Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    module declaration, Go 1.22 or newer, no dependencies\ncmd/csvstat/main.go       process startup; the only caller of os.Exit\ninternal/cli/             Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int\ninternal/csvio/           reading, trimming, and rejecting CSV input\ninternal/profile/         null rule, type inference, counts, statistics, frequencies\ninternal/output/          profile and frequency CSV writers\nbin/test.sh               POSIX test runner\n```\n\n## Modules and Boundaries\n\n| Module | Owns | Allowed to use |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cmd/csvstat` | Wiring `os.Args[1:]`, `os.Stdout`, `os.Stderr` into `cli.Run`; calling `os.Exit` | `internal/cli` |\n| `internal/cli` | Argument parsing with a local `flag.FlagSet` (`flag.ContinueOnError`), exit-code mapping, diagnostics, orchestration | `csvio`, `profile`, `output` |\n| `internal/csvio` | Opening and reading the file, trimming, structural rejection | `os`, `encoding/csv` |\n| `internal/profile` | Null classification, typing, counts, exact arithmetic with `math/big`, frequencies | `math/big`, `time`, `sort` |\n| `internal/output` | CSV writing through `encoding/csv` with `\\n` line endings | `encoding/csv` |\n\nPersistence, configuration, file-store, and external-service boundaries: none. The only file access is reading the one input path named on the command line, owned by `internal/csvio`.\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Only `main` calls `os.Exit`; `Run` returns the exit code so tests drive it with `bytes.Buffer` writers.\n- Nothing is written to standard output until the whole input has been read and validated, so a failing command writes nothing to standard output.\n- Sums, means, and medians are exact (`big.Rat`) and rounded once, half away from zero, when written.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file and embeds no answer.\n- Invalid UTF-8 is accepted; blank lines are skipped by `encoding/csv`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-module-layout ===\nIntent: The application root is a Go module of Go 1.22 or newer with the csvstat main package and the cli package present.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport re\n\nassert os.path.isfile(\"go.mod\")\nwith open(\"go.mod\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n    gomod = handle.read()\nmatch = re.search(r\"^go\\s+(\\d+)\\.(\\d+)\", gomod, re.MULTILINE)\nassert match is not None\nmajor, minor = int(match.group(1)), int(match.group(2))\nassert (major, minor) >= (1, 22)\nassert os.path.isfile(os.path.join(\"cmd\", \"csvstat\", \"main.go\"))\nassert os.path.isdir(os.path.join(\"internal\", \"cli\"))\n=== END AC arch-module-layout ===\n\n=== AC arch-no-dependencies ===\nIntent: go.mod declares no third-party requirement, so the standard library is the only dependency.\n\nimport re\n\nwith open(\"go.mod\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n    gomod = handle.read()\nassert re.search(r\"^\\s*require\\b\", gomod, re.MULTILINE) is None\n=== END AC arch-no-dependencies ===\n\n=== AC arch-cli-run-compiles ===\nIntent: The module builds and vets cleanly, so cli.Run and the main package compile together.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nbuild = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"build\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(build.stdout)\nprint(build.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert build.returncode == 0\n\nvet = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"vet\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(vet.stdout)\nprint(vet.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert vet.returncode == 0\n=== END AC arch-cli-run-compiles ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Standard library only; no third-party modules.\n- Only `main` calls `os.Exit`.\n- Do not modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute any file under `sources/`.\n- Do not read `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file from application code, and do not hardcode a statistic, a type, or a value list.\n- Diagnostics go to standard error and are never part of the output contract.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Build.md ===\n# FEATURE: Build Executable\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | The csvstat executable builds from the application root with go build using only the standard library. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | csvstat executable |\n| Consumes    | internal/cli.Run |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`go build -o <path> ./cmd/csvstat` succeeds from the application root and produces the `csvstat` executable. `main` passes `os.Args[1:]`, `os.Stdout`, and `os.Stderr` to `cli.Run` and exits with the returned code. The supplied grader builds the executable this way.\n\n## Trigger\n\nA developer or the grader runs `go build -o <path> ./cmd/csvstat`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The module is Go 1.22 or newer and imports only the standard library and its own packages.\n- `main` is the only caller of `os.Exit`.\n- The built binary is runnable and exits with the code `cli.Run` returns.\n\n## Programmatic ===\n# FEATURE: CSV Reader\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Read a CSV file with encoding/csv, taking the first record as the header and trimming every header name and value. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | internal/csvio.Read |\n| Consumes    | None |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`internal/csvio` reads the file as RFC 4180 CSV with `encoding/csv`. The first record is the header. Every header name and every value is trimmed of leading and trailing spaces before any other rule applies. Structural rejection (empty file, bad header, ragged record) belongs to the InputRejection feature; this feature supplies the parsed, trimmed records and their line numbers.\n\n## Interface\n\n`csvio.Read(path string) (*Table, error)` where `Table` carries:\n\n- `Header []string`: trimmed header names.\n- `Rows [][]string`: trimmed data records in file order.\n- `Lines []int`: the 1-based starting line of each data record (from `csv.Reader.FieldPos`), kept for diagnostics.\n\nThe reader sets `FieldsPerRecord = -1` so that a field-count mismatch is detected by the application and not by `encoding/csv`, which lets the caller name the offending line. `LazyQuotes` stays false. A quoted field containing a comma, a quote, or a newline is returned as one field.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Trim spaces from header names and values, for example a value `  Globex  ` becomes `Globex`.\n- A quoted field that contains a comma stays one field.\n- Blank lines are skipped by `encoding/csv`.\n- Invalid UTF-8 bytes are read and not rejected.\n- Failure to open or read the file is returned as an error distinguishable from a CSV syntax error, so the caller can map it to exit 1 or exit 3.\n\n## Required Project Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/csvio` using files the tests create. Acceptance runs them by exact name.\n\n- `TestReadTrimsHeaderAndValues`: padded header names and padded values come back trimmed.\n- `TestReadKeepsQuotedCommaInOneField`: a quoted field containing a comma is one field.\n- `TestReadReturnsRecordLines`: `Lines` has one entry per data record and reflects the file line.\n- `TestReadAcceptsInvalidUTF8`: a file with a byte that is not valid UTF-8 is read without error.\n- `TestReadMissingFileIsOpenError`: a missing path returns an error that is not a CSV parse error.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC reader-trims ===\nIntent: Header names and values are trimmed of leading and trailing spaces.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadTrimsHeaderAndValues\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-trims ===\n\n=== AC reader-quoted-comma ===\nIntent: A quoted field containing a comma is read as a single field.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadKeepsQuotedCommaInOneField\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-quoted-comma ===\n\n=== AC reader-record-lines ===\nIntent: Each data record keeps its line number for diagnostics.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadReturnsRecordLines\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-record-lines ===\n\n=== AC reader-invalid-utf8 ===\nIntent: A file containing bytes that are not valid UTF-8 is read and not rejected.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadAcceptsInvalidUTF8\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-invalid-utf8 ===\n\n=== AC reader-open-error ===\nIntent: A missing file produces an open error that is distinct from a CSV parse error.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadMissingFileIsOpenError\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-open-error ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Use `encoding/csv` from the standard library only.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file.\n- Invalid UTF-8 is not a rejection condition.\n- Do not modify any file under `sources/`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-InputRejection.md ===\n# FEATURE: Input Rejection\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Reject an empty, malformed, or ragged CSV file with exit 3, nothing on standard output, and one diagnostic line on standard error. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CsvReader.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | exit code 3 for rejected input |\n| Consumes    | internal/csvio.Read |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nAfter reading the file, the application rejects it when any of these holds:\n\n- the file is empty (no header record);\n- a header name is empty after trimming;\n- a header name is repeated;\n- any data record has a different number of fields from the header;\n- `encoding/csv` reports a syntax error (a `csv.ParseError`), such as a bare quote.\n\nA rejected file exits 3, writes nothing to standard output, and writes one line to standard error naming the file and, for a record, its line number. Standard error text is never compared.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Validation completes before anything is written to standard output.\n- The ragged record is identified by the line number the reader recorded for it.\n- A file with a header and no data records is valid and is not rejected.\n- An open or read failure is not a rejection; it is exit 1 (FEATURE-ReadFailure.md).\n\n## Programmatic FEATURE-ReadFailure.md ===\n# FEATURE: Read Failure\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Report a file that cannot be opened or read, | internal/csvio.Read |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nWhen the file path cannot be opened or read, the application exits 1, writes nothing to standard output, and writes one diagnostic line to standard error naming the path. This covers a missing file, a permission failure, and a path that names a directory. The application must not panic; a Go panic exits 2, which would be misreported as a usage error.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- An open failure from `os.Open` maps to exit 1.\n- A read failure after opening, such as reading a directory, maps to exit 1.\n- A valid path with a malformed argument list is a usage error (exit 2), decided before the file is touched.\n- A CSV syntax or structure problem is a rejection (exit 3), not exit 1.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC readfail-missing-file ===\nIntent: A path that does not exist exits 1 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport os\nimport os.path.isdir(work)\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(work)\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 1\n    assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC readfail-directory ===\n\n=== AC readfail-distinct-from-rejection ===\nIntent: An unreadable path exits with a different code from a rejected file, so exit 1 and exit 3 are not conflated.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: CsvStat\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, boundaries, and technical decisions for the csvstat command-line CSV column profiler. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | go.mod, cmd/csvstat, internal/cli.Run |\n| Consumes    | None |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`csvstat` is a single-binary Go command-line tool. It reads one CSV file, infers a type for each column, and writes either a profile table or a frequency list to standard output as CSV. Diagnostics go to standard error. Exit codes are 0 (success), 1 (operational failure), 2 (usage error), and 3 (rejected input).\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`, which remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | Every package; no third-party modules and no `require` line in `go.mod` |\n| go test and go vet | The project suite behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| CSV (RFC 4180, `encoding/csv`) | Reading input and writing output |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` |\n\nThere is no database and no persistent state, so there is no `DATABASE.md`.\n\n## Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    module declaration, Go 1.22 or newer, no dependencies\ncmd/csvstat/main.go       process startup; the only caller of os.Exit\ninternal/cli/             Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int\ninternal/csvio/           reading, trimming, and rejecting CSV input\ninternal/profile/         null rule, type inference, counts, statistics, frequencies\ninternal/output/          profile and frequency CSV writers\nbin/test.sh               POSIX test runner\n```\n\n## Modules and Boundaries\n\n| Module | Owns | Allowed to use |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cmd/csvstat` | Wiring `os.Args[1:]`, `os.Stdout`, `os.Stderr` into `cli.Run`; calling `os.Exit` | `internal/cli` |\n| `internal/cli` | Argument parsing with a local `flag.FlagSet` (`flag.ContinueOnError`), exit-code mapping, diagnostics, orchestration | `csvio`, `profile`, `output` |\n| `internal/csvio` | Opening and reading the file, trimming, structural rejection | `os`, `encoding/csv` |\n| `internal/profile` | Null classification, typing, counts, exact arithmetic with `math/big`, frequencies | `math/big`, `time`, `sort` |\n| `internal/output` | CSV writing through `encoding/csv` with `\\n` line endings | `encoding/csv` |\n\nPersistence, configuration, file-store, and external-service boundaries: none. The only file access is reading the one input path named on the command line, owned by `internal/csvio`.\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Only `main` calls `os.Exit`; `Run` returns the exit code so tests drive it with `bytes.Buffer` writers.\n- Nothing is written to standard output until the whole input has been read and validated, so a failing command writes nothing to standard output.\n- Sums, means, and medians are exact (`big.Rat`) and rounded once, half away from zero, when written.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file and embeds no answer.\n- Invalid UTF-8 is accepted; blank lines are skipped by `encoding/csv`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-module-layout ===\nIntent: The application root is a Go module of Go 1.22 or newer with the csvstat main package and the cli package present.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport re\n\nassert os.path.isfile(\"go.mod\")\nwith open(\"go.mod\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n    gomod = handle.read()\nmatch = re.search(r\"^go\\s+(\\d+)\\.(\\d+)\", gomod, re.MULTILINE)\nassert match is not None\nmajor, minor = int(match.group(1)), int(match.group(2))\nassert (major, minor) >= (1, 22)\nassert os.path.isfile(os.path.join(\"cmd\", \"csvstat\", \"main.go\"))\nassert os.path.isdir(os.path.join(\"internal\", \"cli\"))\n=== END AC arch-module-layout ===\n\n=== AC arch-no-dependencies ===\nIntent: go.mod declares no third-party requirement, so the standard library is the only dependency.\n\nimport re\n\nwith open(\"go.mod\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n    gomod = handle.read()\nassert re.search(r\"^\\s*require\\b\", gomod, re.MULTILINE) is None\n=== END AC arch-no-dependencies ===\n\n=== AC arch-cli-run-compiles ===\nIntent: The module builds and vets cleanly, so cli.Run and the main package compile together.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nbuild = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"build\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(build.stdout)\nprint(build.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert build.returncode == 0\n\nvet = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"vet\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(vet.stdout)\nprint(vet.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert vet.returncode == 0\n=== END AC arch-cli-run-compiles ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Standard library only; ===\n# FEATURE: CSV Reader\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Read a CSV file with encoding/csv, taking the first record as the header and trimming every header name and value. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | internal/csvio.Read |\n| Consumes    | None |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`internal/csvio` reads the file as RFC 4180 CSV with `encoding/csv`. The first record is the header. Every header name and every value is trimmed of leading and trailing spaces before any other rule applies. Structural rejection (empty file, bad header, ragged record) belongs to the InputRejection feature; this feature supplies the parsed, trimmed records and their line numbers.\n\n## Interface\n\n`csvio.Read(path string) (*Table, error)` where `Table` carries:\n\n- `Header []string`: trimmed header names.\n- `Rows [][]string`: trimmed data records in file order.\n- `Lines []int`: the 1-based starting line of each data record (from `csv.Reader.FieldPos`), kept for diagnostics.\n\nThe reader sets `FieldsPerRecord = -1` so that a field-count mismatch is detected by the application and not by `encoding/csv`, which lets the caller name the offending line. `LazyQuotes` stays false. A quoted field containing a comma, a quote, or a newline is returned as one field.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Trim spaces from header names and values, for example a value `  Globex  ` becomes `Globex`.\n- A quoted field that contains a comma stays one field.\n- Blank lines are skipped by `encoding/csv`.\n- Invalid UTF-8 bytes are read and not rejected.\n- Failure to open or read the file is returned as an error distinguishable from a CSV syntax error, so the caller can map it to exit 1 or exit 3.\n\n## Required Project Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/csvio` using files the tests create. Acceptance runs them by exact name.\n\n- `TestReadTrimsHeaderAndValues`: padded header names and padded values come back trimmed.\n- `TestReadKeepsQuotedCommaInOneField`: a quoted field containing a comma is one field.\n- `TestReadReturnsRecordLines`: `Lines` has one entry per data record and reflects the file line.\n- `TestReadAcceptsInvalidUTF8`: a file with a byte that is not valid UTF-8 is read without error.\n- `TestReadMissingFileIsOpenError`: a missing path returns an error that is not a CSV parse error.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC reader-trims ===\nIntent: Header names and values are trimmed of leading and trailing spaces.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadTrimsHeaderAndValues\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-trims ===\n\n=== AC reader-quoted-comma ===\nIntent: A quoted field containing a comma is read as a single field.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadKeepsQuotedCommaInOneField\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-quoted-comma ===\n\n=== AC reader-record-lines ===\nIntent: Each data record keeps its line number for diagnostics.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadReturnsRecordLines\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-record-lines ===\n\n=== AC reader-invalid-utf8 ===\nIntent: A file containing bytes that are not valid UTF-8 is read and not rejected.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadAcceptsInvalidUTF8\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-invalid-utf8 ===\n\n=== AC reader-open-error ===\nIntent: A missing file produces an open error that is distinct from a CSV parse error.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestReadMissingFileIsOpenError\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC reader-open-error ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Use `encoding/csv` from the standard library only.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file.\n- Invalid UTF-8 is not a rejection condition.\n- Do not modify any file under `sources/`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-InputRejection.md ===\n# FEATURE: Input Rejection\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Reject an empty, malformed, or ragged CSV file with exit 3, nothing on standard output, and one diagnostic line on standard error. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CsvReader.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | exit code 3 for rejected input |\n| Consumes    | internal/csvio.Read |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nAfter reading the file, the application rejects it when any of these holds:\n\n- the file is empty (no header record);\n- a header name is empty after trimming;\n- a header name is repeated;\n- any data record has a different number of fields from the header;\n- `encoding/csv` reports a syntax error (a `csv.ParseError`), such as a bare quote.\n\nA rejected file exits 3, writes nothing to standard output, and writes one line to standard error naming the file and, for a record, its line number. Standard error text is never compared.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Validation completes before anything is written to standard output.\n- The ragged record is identified by the line number the reader recorded for it.\n- A file with a header and no data records is valid and is not rejected.\n- An open or read failure is not a rejection; it is exit 1 (FEATURE-ReadFailure.md).\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC reject-ragged-record ===\nIntent: A record with more fields than the header exits 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport os\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\npath = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(\"sources\", \"ragged.csv\"))\nassert os.path.isfile(path)\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(path)\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 3\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC reject-ragged-record ===\n\n=== AC reject-short-record ===\nIntent: A record with fewer fields than the header exits 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as work:\n    path = os.path.join(work, \"short.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\", newline=\"\") as handle:\n        handle.write(\"a,b,c\\n1,2,3\\n4,5\\n\")\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(path)\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 3\n    assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC reject-short-record ===\n\n=== AC reject-empty-file ===\nIntent: An empty file exits 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as work:\n    path = os.path.join(work, \"empty.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"wb\"):\n        pass\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(path)\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 3\n    assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC reject-empty-file ===\n\n=== AC reject-empty-header-name ===\nIntent: A header with an empty name exits 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as work:\n    path = os.path.join(work, \"emptyname.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\", newline=\"\") as handle:\n        handle.write(\"a,,c\\n1,2,3\\n\")\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(path)\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 3\n    assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC reject-empty-header-name ===\n\n=== AC reject-repeated-header-name ===\nIntent: A header with a repeated name, including one that differs only by padding, exits 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport os\nimport reject-header-only-not-rejected ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A rejected file writes nothing to standard output.\n- Diagnostics go to standard error only and are never compared.\n- The list of rejection conditions is exhaustive; invalid UTF-8 is not one of them.\n- Do not modify any file under `sources/`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ReadFailure.md ===\n# FEATURE: Read Failure\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Report a file that cannot be opened or read, including a directory path, with exit 1 and nothing on standard output. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CsvReader.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | exit code 1 for operational failure |\n| Consumes    | internal/csvio.Read |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nWhen the file path cannot be opened or read, the application exits 1, writes nothing to standard output, and writes one diagnostic line to standard error naming the path. This covers a missing file, a permission failure, and a path that names a directory. The application must not panic; a Go panic exits 2, which would be misreported as a usage error.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- An open failure from `os.Open` maps to exit 1.\n- A read failure after opening, such as reading a directory, maps to exit 1.\n- A valid path with a malformed argument list is a usage error (exit 2), decided before the file is touched.\n- A CSV syntax or structure problem is a rejection (exit 3), not exit 1.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC readfail-missing-file ===\nIntent: A path that does not exist exits 1 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as work:\n    path = os.path.join(work, \"no-such-file.csv\")\n    assert not os.path.exists(path)\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(path)\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 1\n    assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC readfail-missing-file ===\n\n=== AC readfail-directory ===\nIntent: A path naming a directory exits 1 with nothing on standard output and does not panic.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as work:\n    assert os.path.isdir(work)\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(work)\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 1\n    assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC readfail-directory ===\n\n=== AC readfail-distinct-from-rejection ===\nIntent: An unreadable path exits with a different code from a rejected file, so exit 1 and exit 3 are not conflated.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nragged = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(\"sources\", \"ragged.csv\"))\nassert os.path.isfile(ragged)\nrejected_code, rejected_out, rejected_err = cli_runner(ragged)\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as work:\n    missing = os.path.join(work, \"absent.csv\")\n    missing_code, missing_out, missing_err = cli_runner(missing)\n\nprint(rejected_err)\nprint(missing_err)\nassert missing_code != rejected_code\nassert missing_code == 1\nassert rejected_code == 3\n=== END AC readfail-distinct-from-rejection ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A read failure writes nothing to standard output.\n- The application never panics on an unreadable path.\n- Diagnostics go to standard error only and are never compared.\n- Do not modify any file under `sources/`.\n=== END ARTIFACT
